@@122681406 <h> Heat-proofing our schools <p> The start of this year 's dry season felt like d ? j ? vu : Classes in nearly half of the schools in Metro Manila were suspended due to soaring temperatures . The same thing happened last year , but the government should not normalize this as the only solution to problems caused by global warming . <p> Extreme weather events -- from super typhoons to droughts -- are a reality today , but they are even more so for the Philippines , whose geographical location makes it among the most vulnerable countries to climate change . The government has a National Climate Action Plan ( 2011-2028 ) , which lays out the policy framework for climate change adaptation and mitigation efforts , but there is no comprehensive heat action plan that stakeholders can turn to for reference . <p> One of the local governments that responded to last year 's heat wave crisis was Manila , which said it would formulate a heat action plan matrix to guide city officials in implementing specific local measures at each heat alert level . <p> Outside @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ whose work requires them to be exposed to the outdoors such as construction workers and delivery riders . This makes having a national heat action plan even more urgent because this is not a one-time crisis that affects only specific areas or sectors . <h> Historical weather data <p> The report by the nonprofit group Climate Central , which ranked the Philippines third in Asia with the most " unusual heat " caused by climate change within the last three months , should serve as a warning on what to expect in the months ahead . It stated that the country experienced temperatures reaching Climate Shift Index level 2 or higher for 74 days . This means that temperatures during those days were most likely due to the effects of climate change and , considering historical weather data , it will not be any better as the weather heats up during the summer . <p> The Philippine Atmospheric , Geophysical and Astronomical Services Administration 's ( Pagasa ) heat index has five categories : not hazardous ( 27 degrees Celsius and below ) ; caution ( 27-32 degrees Celsius ) @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ ( 42-51 degrees Celsius ) ; and extreme danger ( 52 degrees Celsius and above ) . Pagasa 's advisories have been helpful for local governments and schools , which are encouraged to suspend classes once the heat index reaches danger levels , to anticipate temperatures , and to make decisions ahead of time . This month 's class suspensions were due to the heat index reaching danger levels , but mitigation should go beyond this . <h> Major redesign <p> An article by Manuel B. Garcia ( " Physical school closures as a public health response to high heat index in the Philippines : a critical perspective " ) published in the Journal of Public Health and Emergency in September last year suggested that the government must invest in upgraded school facilities that could withstand extreme weather conditions such as green building materials and designs that could naturally regulate temperature . <p> Article continues after this advertisement <p> Extreme heat conditions can cause heat-related illnesses such as heat exhaustion and heatstroke and make it difficult for students to focus during classes . Not all schools , especially public ones @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ in constructing classrooms to provide students with an environment conducive to learning . However , this entails a major redesign of school buildings that might not be considered a priority for education spending . <p> Article continues after this advertisement <p> Garcia also emphasized that , aside from upgrading school facilities , the government must also invest in technology to ensure that learning is not interrupted when classes shift from in-person to remote sessions . <h> Climate reality <p> These issues on technology and inadequate school buildings are not exactly new . That they remain largely unaddressed points to the government 's failure that has only been highlighted by the many corruption cases -- from overpriced laptops to ghost students , among others -- plaguing the Department of Education . The department 's budget lost to graft and corruption could have built modern school buildings and bought much-needed gadgets for students and teachers . <p> Article continues after this advertisement <p> Millions of students across the country were affected by last year 's class suspensions and the same thing could happen again this year . However , students ca n't @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ learning crisis . <p> To be fair , the government has taken various steps such as blended learning and the gradual return to the traditional school calendar but it needs long-term solutions that could withstand weather challenges and adapt to today 's climate reality . <p> Earlier this month , Malaca ? ang said it was working with the Department of Health and Pagasa to craft an action plan for heat waves -- the latest word is that they were still collating data . Hopefully , it would not take another year and several class suspensions before the government can finally get its act together . 
@@122681423 <h> Mining ban in Palawan : Triumph for nature <p> 10:03 PM March 22 , 2025 <p> The moratorium aims to protect what remains of the country 's " last ecological frontier " where mining explorations and operations are ongoing , as shown in photo taken in Aborlan. -- Photos by Geraldford Ticke and Environmental Legal Assistance Center <p> PUERTO PRINCESA CITY , PALAWAN , Philippines -- A hard-fought environmental battle has finally paid off recently as the Palawan provincial government officially approved Ordinance No. 3646 , declaring a 50-year extendable moratorium on the issuance of endorsements for all large and small-scale mining applications . <p> This historic decision of the provincial board on March 5 is a major victory for environmental advocates who have long opposed destructive mining operations in the country 's last ecological frontier . <p> Article continues after this advertisement <p> " Our forests can finally breathe , but we have to ensure that this policy is enforced . The next 50 years will be a time of recovery and continued monitoring , " shared Save Palawan Movement ( SPM ) coconvenor Cynthia Sumagaysay-Del Rosario . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the beginning of a broader fight . <p> The moratorium covers not just new mining ventures but also applications for exploration permits , mineral agreements , and financial or technical assistance agreements . <p> For antimining advocates like Sumagaysay-Del Rosario , this ordinance is a hard-earned milestone in the continuing fight to preserve Palawan 's rich biodiversity and protect its communities from the damaging effects of extractive industries . <p> Article continues after this advertisement <p> The SPM and other nongovernment organizations ( NGOs ) , along with civil society organizations ( CSOs ) and religious leaders have been fighting against large-scale mining operations in Palawan . <h> Early push <p> The province , composed of more than 1,700 islands , is a long and narrow stretch of around 434 kilometers with maximum width of 39 km . Its land area , including the capital Puerto Princesa City , comprises 1,440,496 hectares , 70 percent of which is covered with natural forests that boasts of coastal and marine ecosystem composed of mangrove swamps , coral reefs , pristine natural beauty and rich biodiversity , earning it the tag as the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ continues after this advertisement <p> The Environmental Legal Assistance Center ( Elac ) and other advocacy groups had already pushed for a 25-year small-scale mining moratorium in 2008 , though it was not enacted as an ordinance . Despite setbacks , environmental defenders did not relent . <p> For other Palawan environmental protection advocates , the actual battle started years before as NGOs have been pressing against the establishment of new mining operations in the province . <p> Lawyer Grizelda Mayo-Anda , Elac executive director , said that back in 2008 , the Palawan provincial board also passed a resolution for a 25-year moratorium on small-scale mining . <p> Mayo-Anda , however , said that former Vice Gov . David Ponce de Leon , along with other lawyers , reiterated that an ordinance for the moratorium would not be possible . <p> " But we were happy during that time that at least they came out with a resolution , although we would have wanted an ordinance , " Mayo-Anda told the Inquirer . <p> She also said that Palawan 's environment scored another victory when the late former President @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ on new mining operations . It was short-lived though after former President Rodrigo Duterte , who succeeded Aquino in 2016 , lifted it . <p> But the proposed ordinance itself took dramatic turns as NGOs , CSOs and religious organizations had to exert extra effort to lobby for its immediate approval . <p> A move praised by members of Save Palawan Movement . <h> People 's voice <p> The fight to achieve this moratorium began long before its passage . Nearly a year ago , the provincial government initiated the Provincial Stakeholders Congress on Mining and the Environment ( PSCME ) in April , gathering various groups to discuss the critical impacts of mining . <p> What started as a forum to assess the province 's mining policies quickly became a strong demonstration of the people 's overwhelming opposition to mining expansion in Palawan . <p> Gov . Victorino Dennis Socrates admitted that prior to the summit , he had no clear stance on a moratorium . However , after hearing testimonies from communities , scientists and environmentalists about the devastation caused by mining , his perspective changed . The @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ to ignore . <p> In September 2024 , Socrates , in his State of the Province Address , echoed the urgent call to enact a mining moratorium . However , legislative hurdles arose when the Provincial Legal Office initially argued that such an ordinance exceeded the powers of the provincial board . This legal challenge forced revisions , but instead of weakening the moratorium , it was strengthened -- extending the initial 25-year proposal to a 50-year ban on new mining applications . <p> With the ordinance seemingly stalled , advocates mobilized once more . NGOs launched a signature campaign , while the Catholic Church amplified the call for action . <p> In a show of unity , Palawan 's three bishops -- Socrates Mesiona of Puerto Princesa , Broderick Pabillo of Taytay and his predecessor , Edgardo Juanich -- issued a pastoral letter urging lawmakers to heed the public 's call . The campaign gained further momentum as thousands of signatures poured in , alongside social media campaigns , rallies and petitions . <p> The moratorium aims to protect what remains of the country 's " last ecological frontier @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ shown in photo taken in Narra . <h> Real dangers <p> Nature itself seemed to lend its voice to the cause . As debates continued , heavy flooding triggered by deforestation-related factors underscored the real dangers of environmental destruction . <p> The widespread damage became a stark reminder of why mining must be halted to protect Palawan 's fragile ecosystems . <p> In a recent press conference , lawyer Teodoro Jose Matta , executive director of the Palawan Council for Sustainable Development , said the council conducted a study back in 2022 on the causes of flooding in the municipality of Brooke 's Point -- one of the areas severely affected by climate change and forest loss , the findings of which he said were also sent to the governor 's office with recommendations for measures to address and further prevent future flooding . <p> Matta said one key feature of the study and research was that in Brooke 's Point alone , 4,000 ha of forest cover are lost every year to kaingin ( slash-and-burn farming ) , human settlements and agricultural expansion . <p> " If you expand @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ " he noted . <p> He cited poverty as one of the major causes of deforestation . He said there were enough socioeconomic programs lined up to uplift the grassroots communities yet these had not been implemented , leading people to fall back to the hinterlands because there was no viable income for them in urban centers . <p> " This is a far more bigger threat . Imagine 4,000 ha times 24 municipalities , even if you only count the mainland municipalities where most of our forests are , " Matta explained . <p> " If we are not addressing the main cause of deforestation , the main cause of floods , we have grown blind and deaf to the clamor of Palawe ? os for a better life , " he added . <p> Governor Socrates signed Ordinance No. 3646 on March 13 , making history in Palawan 's environmental movement . The signing took place at the old Governor 's Residence , using the very desk once occupied by his father , a staunch environmentalist , who had once declared that protecting Palawan 's environment was " @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Board Member Arnel Abrina , an indigenous peoples leader and one of the ordinance 's main authors , acknowledged that it was an uphill battle . <p> " At first , we thought it was nearly impossible . But with the people 's support -- and even nature helping us -- it finally happened , " he said . <p> Mayo-Anda , however , said that while they recognize other causes of deforestation such as illegal logging , kaingin farming and other agricultural activities , and encroachments , there was also a need for transparency . <p> She said that compared to nickel mining , kaingin farming removes only the top soil and , therefore , the chances of the land reverting back to its original state is high . <p> " So we respect that , we agree that there are also other problems and not only mining that causes deforestation , " Mayo-Anda said . <p> " But with mining , you remove the forests , and its wildlife habitat , and you remove the top soil . So there is a big world of difference , " @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ antimining advocates remained vigilant as they warned that mining companies would continue to push back . <p> The Philippine Nickel Industry Association ( PNIA ) criticized Palawan 's 50-year mining moratorium , arguing that it exceeds the province 's mandate and undermines both the Constitution and the country 's global competitiveness . <p> The PNIA , in a statement on March 7 , said the ban lacked legal and regulatory basis , disrupted the balance between local autonomy and national oversight , and would discourage investment by creating uncertainty . <p> " By imposing such a blanket ban without sufficient legal foundation or consultation with national stakeholders , it undermines the balance between local autonomy and national oversight , which is an essential element for sustainable and equitable development , " the group said .

@@122689020 <h> Facing unusual heat <p> Scientists from the nonprofit group Climate Central have ranked the Philippines third in Asia among countries with the most " unusual heat " caused by climate change within the last three months , experiencing temperatures reaching Climate Shift Index ( CSI ) level 2 or higher . That means temperatures during those days were most likely due to the effects of climate change . <p> The CSI is a system developed by Climate Central that quantifies the local influence of climate change on a country 's daily temperatures . The higher the level , the greater the influence of climate change detected . <p> The study noted that 10 out of 51 countries in Asia recorded more than 30 days of temperatures reaching CSI level 2 or higher . Brunei Darussalam ranked first with 83 days , while Maldives was second with 81. 74 days put the Philippines in third , followed by Sri Lanka , Timor-Leste , Malaysia , Singapore , Yemen , and Myanmar . <p> " Over the last three months , nearly 554 million people across 10 countries in Asia experienced @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ for at least one-third of the season , " Climate Central said . <p> The report added that " more than 45 million people across the continent were exposed to at least 30 risky heat days that were added by climate change . " In the Philippines , almost 200,000 Filipinos were said to have been exposed out of the 116 million total population . <p> Scientists also found that among the world 's 38 ' megacities ' -- those with a population of over 10 million -- Manila was included in the 11 areas reported to have " endured heat that was strongly influenced by climate change . " <p> Risky heat refers to temperatures hotter than 90 percent of that observed in a local area from 1991 to 2020 . " Heat-related health risks rise when temperatures rise above this local threshold , " Climate Central pointed out . Risky heat is here to stay , at least until humanity can unite and come up with a solution that can reverse the climate change it has caused . While there may not be much Filipinos can do , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , especially in how our homes , communities , towns and cities are built or improved , so we can face the heat without endangering our health and well-being in this changing world. *

Rules on indoor ventilation can be tightened for schools , workplaces and commercial areas . <p> Seeing the disruptive impact of high heat , no government can afford inaction . The problem must be given the urgent attention that it deserves .

@@122778901 <h> Understanding the global cacao shortage and how it impacts the Philippines <p> We checked up on a local cacao farmer and our leading artisanal chocolatiers to ask what exactly is happening , how they are coping , and what should be done to avoid this in the future <p> Chocolate is one of the most popular food types and flavours in the world . Cocoa , its main ingredient , comes from the cacao fruit whose seeds are fermented , dried , roasted , and ground to make cocoa , which is then made into different forms of chocolate . In Latin America ( or what was known as ancient Mesoamerica ) where the cacao tree was first domesticated five thousand years ago , chocolate is not only consumed as food but also drank in rituals and for medical purposes . They also use it to flavour savoury sauces , such as the Mexican mol ? which is thick and complex . Still , in most parts of the world , chocolate is best known as a sweet , after-meal treat , a decadent dessert to perk one up @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ only because it tastes divine , but is proven to make us feel good too . <p> However , its very existence seems to be threatened by the current global cacao shortage , causing panic within the chocolate industry . Bacolod-based farmer Chris Fadriga is best known for his dedication to the propagation of what is known to be the best cacao plant variety in the world , the criollo cacao . What is causing this shortage , we ask . " Most of the world 's cacao supply comes from African countries where they have been planting them for centuries , " Fadriga explains . " Because of climate change , crops have been dwindling and farmers are struggling to meet the ever-growing demand for cacao around the world . " <p> This shortage , while alarming , does have its advantages according to Theo &amp; Philo Artisan Chocolates founder Philo Chua , whose innovative chocolate creations include flavours that highlight local ingredients such as barako coffee and green mangoes . " Aside from challenges , I think opportunities also opened up , " he points out . " @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ chocolate prices and domestically made chocolates has narrowed . Previously , local cacao prices had normally been higher , especially for good-quality cacao . Also , companies who had stock were able to supply the demand where others fell short , and that has allowed us to enter new markets . " <p> While many local chocolate artisans use locally grown cacao , they still feel the effects of the global shortage . Christian Valdes of CMV Txokolat admits that it was not immediately felt , but even local prices of raw materials started to creep up . " Initially , our company did not feel the effects as strongly , but because of the decline in global production ; disruptions in trade routes due to geopolitical conflicts ( like the attacks of the container shipping through the Suez Canal causing shipping to be diverted ) further increasing shipping cost and affecting availability ; and other challenges in cacao cultivation like low productivity have significantly increased our raw material costs , particularly for cocoa beans and chocolate , " Valdes enumerates . " Since then we have diversified our offerings @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ business relations and enhanced accessibility to reach a broader market . " <p> Auro Chocolate co-founder and managing director Mark Ocampo also feels the pinch of rising prices , yet he chooses to see the silver lining which is beneficial to our local cacao growers . " On a positive note , the shortage in the African region creates an opportunity for other cacao-producing countries such as the Philippines , to fill the gap in the global market . As a result , new markets are opening up for our partner cacao producer , who can capitalise on the growing demand for high-quality , sustainable cacao . This shift could lead to better economic prospects for the entire cacao industry in the country . " <p> This almost seems perfectly timed since the local cacao industry is experiencing a revival and would benefit from some much-needed attention . " While the cacao industry has been around the Philippines for a long time already , it died down back in the late 80s , " Chua explains . " There 's been a revival in the past decade , but that @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of knowledge and other practices . As such , I think we 're starting from scratch in a lot of ways despite the progress we 've had thus far . New infrastructure , knowledge and practices have to be put back into place for the cacao industry to be at the same level as other countries . " <p> Valdez stresses the need to further educate local growers and producers . " Aside from productivity there is a lack of proper knowledge on the cultivation and maintenance of the crop as well as proper post-harvest processing , " he says , " as well as many issues on the business side of things , like pricing and proper marketing quality standards . Based on my observations , it is struggling to reach its full potential . However , with proper education and the adoption of sustainable farming and proper business practices , we can strengthen the industry and drive long-term growth . " <p> Still , the private sector has picked up on the scent of the local cacao industry 's growing potential , with Fadriga 's criollo nursery working @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ a collaboration with a sustainable farmer who aims to grow criollo on 100 hectares of farmland . Still , many much smaller endeavours are feeling the effect of the currently skyrocketing cacao prices and would hugely benefit from government assistance . " They ( the government ) can start with providing farmers with mechanical dryers , " Fadriga suggests . " When cacao seeds are not dried properly , they develop mould which is a huge loss to farmers . The lack of modern technology in most farms puts many local farmers at a disadvantage . " Ocampo believes that assistance should not only be limited to those who are just starting out but also for businesses that are already contributing to the economy . " We believe that both local and national governments should prioritise enhancing training programs and providing financial support to farmers , as well as funding research initiatives , " he stresses . " Moreover , it is important to offer additional assistance to businesses already making meaningful strides in addressing these challenges and driving positive change within the sector . " <p> While the global @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ farmers , balance needs to be regained for the entire industry to benefit . Knowledge is truly power , and as cacao producers and manufacturers continue to struggle through the crisis , learnings must be applied quickly and for the collective to act swiftly as a whole . " For manufacturers , it 's important to diversify their supply . Currently , much of the cacao supply in the Philippines can be found in Davao , but it 's good to also look at other areas outside Davao . An extra step , is to form relationships with farmers and also invest in the farmer to not only secure their supply but also get better quality output , " advises Chua . <p> Valdes recommends maximising the application and potential of cacao to promote self-sufficiency , for example , " a citrus fruit can be used in multiple applications such as syrups , candied fruits , dehydrated components , flavouring , thickener , and infusions . The coffee and teas that we use in our products go into creating organic fertilisers . " Ocampo offers a reality check and states @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ still have a long way to go . " We have been proactive in supporting local cacao farmers , improving sustainable methods , and investing in sustainable practices . But , we believe that for Filipino cacao manufacturers to be truly self-sufficient in the future , more immediate , coordinated action is needed from both the public and private sectors to address these issues effectively and at scale . @@122837918 <h> REVIEW : Out of Mindanao , with love <p> The memoir of Tony La Vi ? a , is not for the faint-hearted . It is for the curious , eager , and inquiring : the reader will be amply rewarded by interesting narratives and inspiring themes in " Ransomed by Love : A Happy Changemaker 's Unfinished Journey . " With his clear articulation of rich experiences , Tony 's memoire provides wholesome reading -- comforting , hopeful and uplifting . ( I read it straight over two days : I was struck by the narrative beauty and simplicity , brought around by a world tour , and came back to re-read many parts to refresh my thoughts . ) <p> I first knew Tony in 2003 and got to work with him in later years . I know him better now through this book . He has plotted his path from childhood since 1959 to the present , 2024 , with vignettes from his birthplace in Cagayan de Oro to other places in his homeland , and the various countries he has visited throughout the world in @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ will discover more about Tony as the pages go by , and his simple and effective writing is a joy to read from cover to end . " I am a son of Mindanao " -- the starting line of the book -- is short , clear and emphatic . The reader will be on an enjoyable ride with him during his journey , listening and understanding him intently , especially when he shares with us his inner thoughts . <p> Out of Mindanao , where he was born and raised , Tony went out to the world to love and be loved . <p> Various lenses and gifts in viewing Tony <p> Tony can be seen through various lenses over the past 65 years : ( i ) the curious student in philosophy and law , who with his PhD , pursued a career in climate change that defined his life 's journey ; ( ii ) a filial son bonding with his lovely mother , who he is clearly proud of ( amongst many other things , she was a trailblazer and the first woman councilor of Cagayan @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ climate change and environmental justice ; his global citizenry ; his social entrepreneurship ; his mentoring of countless people ; and ( iv ) his confronting with the demons during the pandemic -- " the most challenging period of my life " ( p. 183 ) -- including his discovery in 2022 that he had prostate cancer followed by " the near-death experiences NDEs " ( p. 241 ) . With his packed laundry list of life 's chapters , he ends with this homily : that the NDEs " clearly were gifts from God " ( p. 241 ) . The legion of demons took over Tony but God , with his unconditional mercy , saved Tony ( p. 245 ) . <p> Tony 's arduous belief in God and Christianity is the spiritual golden thread running through the book . He notes at the end of the memoire that he is a " very flawed " man ( p. 248 ) . Although his life is an unfinished journey , it continues with God , and with Jesus as hope . While there are many things he does @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the " teacher , activist , and changemaker is happy " ( p. 258 , the final page of his memoire ) . This fitting end marks the salutation he bestows upon us : that one can be surprised by joy even when there are reasons to be sad , and that hope can be found in community and solidarity . <p> He presents his gifts to us through journaling and self-realization . His journaling , sprinkled throughout his memoire , deepens his self-discovery and self-awareness . His journaling in the early days of the Covid-19 pandemic in the Philippines is a valuable record as he prioritizes fears and concerns for his family and his community . His narration of his NDEs makes him realize that he has lived " a very productive and exciting life -- on the edge and no dull moment " ( p. 244 ) . With this self-realization , he wisely advises that everyone suffering from the same traits he has needs to seek help for their own wellbeing and for their loved ones . He reminds us that when we seek help , it @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ us , in solidarity as that is how society thrives in solidarity and in harmony . <p> BOOK SIGNING . Lawyers Romeo Cabarde ( 2nd from left ) and Carlos Isagani Zarate ( R ) , and Prof. Ago Tomas await their turns to have their books autographed by former Law Dean Tony La Vi ? a ( L ) . ARGUILLAS <p> His candor in mapping out his thoughts and conditions are riveting in delivering his gifts because they come straight from the heart with no punches pulled . He has Asperger 's syndrome , and is neurodiverse and bipolar , and admits this readily together with his prostate cancer . This gamechanger enables him to free himself from his shackles and embrace his new life with vitality in his reflective and reflexive practices . <p> Salient themes <p> The book is not all doom and gloom -- far from it . Joy sparkles when we read he is " still a son of @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , my emphasis ) just as he started off his memoire reminding us of his connectivity with Mindanao . The book is not simply about sickness , solitude , and solidarity but of hope and love , as a guide for future generations to build on their experiences . <p> We benefit from Tony 's profound reflections drawn from his life 's journey with love reigning supreme . I can understand why Tony is ransomed by love . In 1 Corinthians 13:13 , St. Paul says " And now these three remain : faith , hope and love ; but the greatest of these is love . " Love surpasses faith and hope . To be ransomed by love is the best way of release from captivity or imprisonment , and unleashing oneself to the fore . <p> There are several salient themes in the memoire , and I select and explain four which are inter-related . <p> Dean Antonio La Vina autographs his book " Ransomed by Love " during its launch in his hometown Cagayan de Oro City on Sunday , 01 December 2024 . MindaNews photo by @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ no peace without justice . Tony says " there can be no peace without justice , there can be no peace if we do not address the inequities in the present and resolve historical injustice " at p. 20 ( emphasis mine ) . He reminds us that 50 years on , this refrain is still his mantra ! This early revelation in his childhood resonates throughout the book . We are peppered with his recollections of remembrances which feed us with curiosity . Where conventional reactors produce 800 to 1,000 megawatts @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , modular reactors produce 300 megawatts or less and microreactors produce no more than 20 megawatts . <p> Article continues after this advertisement <p> Tech giants Amazon and Google are investing in nuclear reactors to get the power they need , as states compete with Big Tech , and each other , in a race for electricity . <h> States are embracing nuclear energy <p> For some state officials , nuclear is a carbon-free source of electricity that helps them meet greenhouse gas-reduction goals . Greg Abbott declared his state is " ready to be No. 1 in advanced nuclear power " as Texas lawmakers consider billions in nuclear power incentives . <p> Michigan lawmakers are considering millions of dollars in incentives to develop and use the reactors , as well as train a nuclear industry workforce . <p> One state over , Indiana lawmakers this month passed legislation to let utilities more quickly seek reimbursement for the cost to build a modular reactor , undoing a decades-old prohibition designed to protect ratepayers from bloated , inefficient or , worse , aborted power projects . <p> In Arizona , lawmakers are considering a utility-backed bill to relax environmental regulations if a utility builds a reactor at the site of a large industrial power user or a retired coal-fired power plant . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ devices face an uncertain future . <p> No modular reactors are operating in the U.S. and a project to build the first , this one in Idaho , was terminated in 2023 , despite getting federal aid . <p> The U.S. Department of Energy last year , under then-President Joe Biden , estimated the U.S. will need an additional 200 gigawatts of new nuclear capacity to keep pace with future power demands and reach net-zero emissions of planet-warming greenhouse gases by 2050 to avoid the worst effects of climate change . <p> The U.S. currently has just under 100 gigawatts of nuclear power operating . More than 30 advanced nuclear projects are under consideration or planned to be in operation by the early 2030s , Nichol of the NEI said , but those would supply just a fraction of the 200 gigawatt goal . <p> Work to produce a modular reactor has drawn billions of dollars in federal subsidies , loan guarantees and more recently tax credits signed into law by Biden . <p> Those have been critical to the nuclear industry , which expects them to survive under President @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . <h> Supply challenges and competition from renewables <p> The U.S. remains without a long-term solution for storing radioactive waste , safety regulators are under pressure from Congress to approve designs and there are serious questions about industry claims that the smaller reactors are efficient , safe and reliable , said Edwin Lyman , director of nuclear power safety at the Union of Concerned Scientists . <p> Plus , Lyman said , " the likelihood that those are going to be deployable and instantly 100% reliable right out of the gate is just not consistent with the history of nuclear power development . And so it 's a much riskier bet . " <p> Nuclear also has competition from renewable energies . <p> Brendan Kochunas , an assistant professor of nuclear engineering at the University of Michigan , said advanced reactors may have a short window to succeed , given the regulatory scrutiny they undergo and the advances in energy storage technologies to make wind and solar power more reliable . <p> The U.S. lacks high-quality concrete- and steel-fabrication design skills necessary to manufacture a nuclear power plant , Kochunas @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and longer timelines , he said . While foreign suppliers could help , there also is the fuel to consider . <p> Kathryn Huff , a former top Energy Department official who is now an associate professor at the University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign , said uranium enrichment capacity in the U.S. and among its allies needs to grow in order to support reactor production .

@@122988197 <h> SPECIAL REPORT : Efforts to fight dengue in Tagum City come up short <p> TAGUM CITY , Davao del Norte ( MindaNews / 30 March ) -- Mosquitoes have been leaving an itch -- literally and figuratively -- to Tagumenyos for more than a year now . <p> The City Health Office of Tagum . MindaNews photo by MANMAN DEJETO <p> Tagum has yet to consistently contain the mosquito-borne dengue since November 27 , 2023 , when the city declared a state of emergency due to rising cases and deaths . <p> The World Health Organization describes dengue as a viral infection transmitted through mosquito bites , usually from aedes mosquito . <p> Statistics shows that the City Health Office ( CHO ) of Tagum recorded 1,278 cases and 12 deaths from January 1 to December 27 , 2023 . In 2024 , 2,302 cases and 11 deaths were already recorded from January 1 to November 30 . That 's an increase by 80.1 percent , with still a month before 2024 ended . <p> Tagum City ( population : 296,202 as of 2020 census ) , has the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , at 2,302 cases . Davao City , the most populous city in Mindanao ( 1,776,949 in 2020 ) recorded 7,165 recorded cases in 2024 . <p> But the rise in the number of dengue cases is apparently not limited to Tagum City . <p> " Several provinces and cities declared a state of calamity because of the significantly increased number of dengue cases this year as compared to previous years , " said Dr. Ma . Delta Aguilar , a pediatrician practicing in Davao City with subspecialization on infectious diseases , during a forum on dengue in Davao City on February 23 . <p> She said the " increased cases are consequences of ... climate change , which leads to increasing temperature , high rainfall , and ... humidity . " <p> In an email sent to MindaNews , the City Epidemiology and Surveillance Unit ( CESU ) of Tagum said most of these dengue cases -- totalling 1,260 -- were children aged 0 to 10 years old . <p> Although declared as a public health emergency in late 2023 , residents here have been vulnerable to dengue for @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ in many areas . Containers -- such as unused and discarded empty bottles , unattended pails , and similar objects -- are filled with stagnant water , a favorite breeding ground for dengue-carrying mosquitoes . Non-flowing canals are a common sight . <p> More than a year after the declaration of state of emergency , Tagumenyos would say there is still a lot to be done . <p> Dengue Situation <p> More than 50 kilometers away from Metro Davao is the city of Tagum , the capital of Davao del Norte . Arriving in Tagum , one will be welcomed by a long stretch of neatly planted palm trees on both sides of the highway . Minutes later is the Magugpo Poblacion area , the innovative and modernized part of the city , with its tall buildings and a series of shopping centers , Mindanao 's longest flyover that stretches over a kilometer , and residential zones that appear to be well-planned . <p> Adjacent to the poblacion is the 1,520-hectare Barangay Visayan Village , which according to the CHO has 368 recorded dengue cases as of this writing . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ non-flowing canals and empty lots , while many residential houses have potted plants . Mosquitoes would be too happy to live and breed in these spots , among the risk factors of dengue , according to the World Health Organization ( WHO ) . <p> It also mentioned that the people or the community 's " population 's knowledge , attitude and practice towards dengue " can also affect dengue risks , as " the exposure is closely related to behaviours such as water storage , plant keeping , and self-protection against mosquito bites . " <p> With the help of the barangay health center staff , MindaNews visited the home of a dengue fatality , a two-year-old who resided in Purok Basakan in Barangay Visayan Village . The youngest of five siblings , " Reyna " was the 10th casualty of the dengue outbreak in the city in 2024 . <p> Unfortunately , both her parents were away when MindaNews came for a visit on December 17 , 2024 , and only her aunt , Irene Warag ( the mother 's sister who serves as the children 's guardian @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ works as a vendor while the father went to Davao de Oro to find work . <p> Warag said that a few days before the child passed away , they noticed the child 's eyes showing signs of distress , as if struggling with dizziness , accompanied by high fever . <p> Warag applied some accupressure to the child 's hands , which seemed to alleviate the dizziness temporarily . <p> However , the child eventually became weak and unwell . <p> " The next morning , we saw her spitting blood . At first we thought it was ok . The second time it happened , we rushed her to the hospital , " Warag said . <p> In less than 24 hours , Reyna 's temperature dropped -- a telltale sign that she was undergoing dengue 's critical phase -- and could not eat properly . Reyna was sent to the ICU , but doctors found out the child already had no pulse and the platelet count was very low . She was declared dead shortly after . <p> Abby Zyra Chua , Barangay Visayan Village health station @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . <p> Dengue awareness tips placed outside the Barangay Health Station of Visayan Village 2 in Tagum City . MindaNews photo by MANMAN DEJETO <p> Going around Reyna 's neighborhood , MindaNews noticed that their pathways have stagnant water trapped in between uneven surfaces of muddy soil , and discarded bottles with either liquor or water inside . <p> Meanwhile , in Barangay La Filipina , even the son of barangay sanitary inspector Evangeline Obero suffered dengue several times . <p> She shared that it started way back in January 2024 , when his son was hospitalized twice in the same month . " <p> " If your child is complaining that he or she has fever , you must remember that fever is a response to an infection happening inside your body , " Pongan said . " <p> A known pediatrician in Davao City , Dr. Diana Dadia , a consultant at the Southern Philippines Medical Center , echoes the same sentiment during @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ them , please be more vigilant . Two days of fever , consult a doctor . " <p> Pongan said the city can not just lift the state of emergency as the dengue cases in Tagum need to be stabilized first . <p> " We just need the community to be with us , " Pongan said . <p> Dr. Dadia has the same advice to communities : " Simply cleaning your house , the elimination of water from your water containers , the following of 5s strategies , everything can be done . @@123010982 <p> Indonesia is n't just a country -- it 's a paradise of endless wonders , with over 17,000 islands waiting to be explored . Beyond the famous beaches of Bali , this vast archipelago hides ancient temples , mystical volcanoes , secret lagoons , and some of the rarest wildlife on Earth . From floating islands to underwater paradises , we 're about to reveal 15 jaw-dropping destinations that most travelers never even hear about . Get ready -- because by the end , you 'll be adding Indonesia to the top of your bucket list ! <p> Tumpak Sewu <h> 15 . Tumpak Sewu <p> Deep in the heart of East Java , hidden beneath a jungle-covered valley , lies one of Indonesia 's most magical waterfalls -- Tumpak Sewu . Its name translates to " A Thousand Waterfalls , " and once you see it , you 'll understand why . This massive 300-foot-high cascade looks like a curtain of silver water , flowing down an emerald-green cliffside . But here 's the twist : unlike Niagara Falls , this one is n't swarmed with tourists -- @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the base is an adventure on its own , leading through slippery jungle trails , misty caves , and narrow bamboo bridges . The best time to visit ? Early morning , when the golden sunrise paints the waterfall in shades of gold and silver -- a sight you 'll never forget . <p> Mount Rinjani <h> 14 . Mount Rinjani <p> Standing tall at 12,224 feet , Mount Rinjani is n't just a mountain -- it 's an active volcano with a giant crater lake inside ! Located on the island of Lombok , this is Indonesia 's most thrilling trek , and only the boldest adventurers dare to climb it . The reward ? A breathtaking blue lake sitting inside the crater of a living volcano . <p> But here 's where it gets even crazier -- at night , you can camp beside the steaming volcanic lake , and if you 're lucky , you might witness small eruptions lighting up the sky like fireworks . It 's an experience that makes you feel like you 're on another planet . <h> 13 . Derawan Islands <p> @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ natural beauty , the Derawan Islands are a perfect hidden gem . Located in East Kalimantan , this archipelago is famous for its crystal-clear waters , exotic marine life , and stunning coral reefs . <p> On Sangalaki Island , you can witness rare giant manta rays , while Kakaban Island offers a unique Jellyfish Lake , where you can swim with stingless jellyfish . Maratua Island and Derawan Island feature serene beaches and luxury overwater bungalows , making them ideal for honeymooners and adventure seekers alike . <p> If you 're searching for an underrated alternative to Raja Ampat with fewer crowds , the Derawan Islands are a must-visit destination . <p> Kawah Ijen <h> 12 . Kawah Ijen <p> If you think you 've seen it all , think again -- because Kawah Ijen is unlike anything on Earth . Located in East Java , this volcano does n't just erupt lava -- it spews electric blue fire ! No , it 's not some sci-fi movie effect ; this is a real-life natural phenomenon caused by burning sulfuric gases at night . <p> But the magic does @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , you 'll be greeted by a giant turquoise crater lake -- the most acidic lake in the world . The contrast between the glowing blue fire and the neon-blue lake makes this one of the most surreal places you 'll ever see . <p> Hiking to Kawah Ijen is an adventure in itself , requiring a challenging three-hour trek through rugged terrain . The eerie glow of the flames at night , combined with the breathtaking sunrise views , makes this a once-in-a-lifetime experience . Along the way , you 'll also encounter sulfur miners , who work under harsh conditions to extract sulfur from the crater -- offering a glimpse into an ancient and grueling profession . For adventure seekers and photographers alike , Kawah Ijen is an unmissable wonder . <p> Wae Rebo <h> 11 . Wae Rebo <p> Hidden deep in the misty mountains of Flores Island , Wae Rebo is a village frozen in time . To reach it , you 'll need to hike four hours through dense jungle , but what awaits you is breathtaking beyond words . <p> Perched at 3,600 feet @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ traditional cone-shaped houses , known as Mbaru Niang , which look like something straight out of a fantasy novel . These unique structures are built entirely from natural materials , preserving the architectural heritage of the Manggarai people . <p> Life in Wae Rebo is simple and deeply rooted in tradition . The villagers welcome visitors with ritual ceremonies , offering a rare opportunity to experience authentic Indonesian culture . The morning mist rolling over the mountains , the peaceful atmosphere , and the star-studded night sky make staying here feel like stepping into another era . If you seek cultural immersion and serenity , Wae Rebo is the perfect escape . <p> Komodo Island <h> 10 . Komodo Island <p> Think dragons are just myths ? Not in Indonesia ! Welcome to Komodo Island , home to the world 's largest living lizards -- the Komodo dragons . These prehistoric giants can grow up to 10 feet long and weigh over 300 pounds . But do n't let their slow movements fool you -- they can run , swim , and even take down a deer in seconds . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ rarest beaches on Earth -- a pink sand beach ! The soft pink hues come from crushed coral , creating a dreamlike shoreline where turquoise waters meet rose-colored sands . This location is the epitome of wild beauty . <p> Lake Toba <h> 9 . Lake Toba <p> A lake so massive it looks like an ocean -- that 's Lake Toba , the world 's largest volcanic lake , covering an area of around 1,130 square kilometers . But here 's the mind-blowing part : this lake was formed by a supervolcano eruption 74,000 years ago -- the largest volcanic event in the last 2 million years ! Scientists believe this eruption caused a global climate change , affecting early human populations . <p> Today , Lake Toba is a serene paradise , perfect for travelers seeking peace and breathtaking natural beauty . Samosir Island , located in the middle of the lake , offers a unique cultural experience with the Batak people , known for their traditional music , dance , and elaborate ceremonies . Visitors can explore ancient Batak villages , relax in natural hot springs , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ hills and crystal-clear waters . Whether you 're taking a boat ride across the vast lake or trekking through the surrounding highlands , Lake Toba is an extraordinary destination that blends history , culture , and nature . <p> Belitung Island <h> 8 . Belitung Island <p> Most people think the best beaches are in Bali -- but Belitung Island is Indonesia 's real hidden treasure . Often called " The Maldives of Indonesia , " Belitung boasts sugar-white sands , giant granite boulders , and waters so clear they look like glass . This lesser-known gem is perfect for those who want a tropical escape without the crowds . <p> Belitung 's most famous attraction , Tanjung Tinggi Beach , features massive granite rock formations that create natural pools and hidden lagoons , making it a paradise for photography lovers . Nearby , Lengkuas Island offers snorkeling and diving experiences , where you can swim among vibrant coral reefs and colorful marine life . For history enthusiasts , the island also has remnants of Dutch colonial heritage and traditional fishing villages where you can sample fresh seafood straight from @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ climbing a historic lighthouse , or simply enjoying the peaceful sunset , Belitung offers a perfect blend of adventure and relaxation . <p> Traditional village , Tana Toraja <h> 7 . Tana Toraja <p> Nestled in the highlands of South Sulawesi , Tana Toraja is a region where ancient traditions and stunning landscapes converge . Known for its elaborate funeral ceremonies , cliffside burial sites , and distinctive boat-shaped houses called Tongkonan , Tana Toraja offers a profound cultural experience . <p> The Torajan people have one of the most unique relationships with death in the world . Instead of immediate burial , families keep deceased loved ones in their homes , treating them as if they are still alive until they can afford a grand funeral , which can take years to prepare . These funerals , which last for days , include buffalo sacrifices , traditional dances , and a final procession to place the deceased in cliffside graves or hanging coffins . <p> Aside from its fascinating culture , Tana Toraja is also a paradise for nature lovers . Surrounded by misty mountains , lush rice terraces @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . Highlights include visiting the Lemo and Londa burial caves , where wooden effigies of the dead , called Tau Tau , stand as guardians of the tombs . If you 're looking for an off-the-beaten-path destination rich in tradition and breathtaking scenery , Tana Toraja should be on your list . <p> Borobudur Temple , Yogyakarta , Java , Indonesia via DepositPhotos <h> 6 . Yogyakarta <p> Often hailed as the cultural heart of Java , Yogyakarta is a city where tradition and modernity harmoniously blend . Home to the magnificent Borobudur and Prambanan temples , Yogyakarta offers a journey through Indonesia 's rich history and spiritual heritage . <p> Borobudur , the world 's largest Buddhist temple , is an architectural masterpiece , featuring thousands of intricate carvings and 72 stupas , each containing a Buddha statue . Watching the sunrise over this sacred monument is an unforgettable experience . Prambanan , a towering Hindu temple complex , enchants visitors with its detailed reliefs depicting stories from the Ramayana epic . <p> Beyond its historical sites , Yogyakarta is a hub for arts and culture . The city @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ ( Wayang Kulit ) , and the lively street food scene along Malioboro Street . Whether you 're exploring underground caves , trying the local dish Gudeg ( jackfruit stew ) , or shopping for handcrafted souvenirs , Yogyakarta is a city that stimulates all the senses . <p> Gili Islands by Mathis Jrdl via Unsplash <h> 5 . Gili Islands <p> Off the northwest coast of Lombok lie the Gili Islands -- Gili Trawangan , Gili Meno , and Gili Air -- each offering its own slice of paradise . With no motorized vehicles allowed , these islands provide a serene escape where bicycles and horse-drawn carts are the primary modes of transport . <p> Gili Trawangan , the largest , is known for its lively nightlife , beachfront bars , and social atmosphere , making it a favorite among backpackers . Gili Meno , the smallest , is a haven for honeymooners , offering seclusion and untouched beauty . Gili Air strikes a perfect balance , with peaceful beaches but enough entertainment options to keep visitors engaged . <p> Underwater , the Gili Islands are just as mesmerizing @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , from sea turtles to exotic fish , making them one of Indonesia 's best spots for snorkeling and diving . Whether you 're enjoying a beachfront yoga session , taking a sunset horseback ride , or exploring shipwrecks underwater , the Gili Islands promise an unforgettable tropical experience . <p> Mount Bromo <h> 4 . Mount Bromo <p> Rising from the Tengger Caldera in East Java , Mount Bromo is an active volcano that offers one of the most breathtaking landscapes in Indonesia . Watching the sunrise over the caldera , with mist swirling around the volcanic peaks , is nothing short of magical . <p> To reach the summit , visitors typically embark on a pre-dawn jeep ride across the Sea of Sand , followed by a short but steep climb to the crater 's edge . The journey is rewarded with panoramic views of Mount Bromo 's steaming crater , surrounded by the dramatic peaks of Mount Semeru and Mount Batok . <p> For adventure seekers , there 's more to explore beyond the main viewpoint . You can hike to King Kong Hill for a quieter @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the Savana Hill , a vast green valley that contrasts beautifully with the volcanic desert . Whether you 're capturing the ethereal scenery through your camera lens or experiencing the thrill of standing on the edge of an active volcano , Mount Bromo is a bucket-list-worthy adventure . <p> Woman at Tegalalang Rice Terrace in Bali <h> 3 . Bali <p> No list of Indonesia 's top destinations would be complete without Bali , often referred to as the " Island of the Gods . " Renowned for its lush rice terraces , sacred temples , vibrant arts scene , and idyllic beaches , Bali offers a diverse array of experiences that cater to every kind of traveler . <p> For adventure seekers , Bali 's world-class surf spots in Kuta , Uluwatu , and Canggu offer thrilling waves , while the island 's dense jungles hide breathtaking waterfalls like Tegenungan and Sekumpul . Those seeking cultural immersion can visit the ancient temples of Tanah Lot , Besakih , and Uluwatu , each offering a glimpse into Bali 's deep spiritual heritage . Beyond the tourist hotspots , Ubud is @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ can practice yoga , indulge in traditional Balinese healing , and explore the scenic Tegalalang Rice Terraces . The island 's nightlife is just as vibrant , with beach clubs like Finns and Potato Head offering sunset views and music-filled nights . <p> Despite its popularity , Bali retains pockets of serenity for those willing to explore . From the black sand beaches of Amed to the untouched beauty of Nusa Penida , Bali continues to enchant visitors with its mystical landscapes and rich traditions . <p> Borobudur <h> 2 . Borobudur <p> Located near Yogyakarta , Borobudur is the world 's largest Buddhist temple and a UNESCO World Heritage site . Built in the 8th and 9th centuries during the Sailendra Dynasty , this architectural masterpiece consists of nine stacked platforms , over 2,600 intricate relief panels , and 504 Buddha statues , each telling a story of Buddhist teachings and philosophy . <p> The temple 's design represents the journey toward enlightenment , with the lower levels symbolizing earthly desires and the uppermost stupa representing Nirvana . As visitors ascend , they transition from the material world to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ 's most mesmerizing experiences is witnessing the sunrise from its summit , as the first light of day illuminates the temple 's stupas against a backdrop of misty forests and volcanoes , including Mount Merapi . <p> Beyond the temple , the surrounding Mendut and Pawon temples offer deeper insights into Indonesia 's Buddhist history . Nearby , the vibrant city of Yogyakarta provides cultural attractions like batik workshops , puppet shows , and the Kraton ( Sultan 's Palace ) , making a visit to Borobudur an unforgettable journey through history and spirituality . <p> Raja Ampat <h> 1 . Raja Ampat <p> At the pinnacle of Indonesia 's most beautiful destinations lies Raja Ampat , an archipelago of over 1,500 islands off the coast of West Papua . Often called the " Last Paradise on Earth , " this remote wonderland is a diver 's utopia , home to over 75% of the world 's coral species and some of the richest marine biodiversity on the planet . <p> Above the surface , the islands ' limestone karst formations , hidden lagoons , and secluded white-sand beaches create @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , with their emerald-green peaks and turquoise waters , offer one of the most iconic views in Indonesia . <p> Raja Ampat remains largely unspoiled , with eco-resorts emphasizing sustainability and conservation . Whether you 're diving into its vibrant underwater world , trekking to panoramic viewpoints , or simply soaking in the untouched beauty , Raja Ampat is a destination that feels like a dream come to life . <p> Indonesia is a land of endless wonders , where each destination tells its own unique story . From the cultural depths of Tana Toraja to the unparalleled beauty of Raja Ampat , this archipelago invites you to embark on journeys that will etch lasting memories . Whether you 're an adventurer , a cultural enthusiast , or someone seeking serenity , Indonesia 's diverse landscapes and rich heritage offer something for everyone . <p> Wonders of Indonesia <p> If you enjoyed this exploration of Indonesia 's top destinations , do n't forget to like , subscribe , and share our content on your social media accounts .
